It has just felt like a constant loss of drive and interest this whole time.\n\nDoctor: Looking back over the last few months, have there been any periods where you felt significantly more energetic, irritable, or unusually high-spirited while still experiencing the voices or feelings of being targeted?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't experienced any periods of high energy or feeling high-spirited at all. If anything, it's been the exact opposite—I've just felt completely drained and lacking any motivation.\n\nDoctor: When you hear these voices or feel targeted, do you experience these thoughts and sounds even during weeks where your mood feels slightly better, or do they only happen when your sadness and lack of motivation are at their absolute worst?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure how to answer that, because I don't really experience distinct waves of sadness—it's just been a constant lack of motivation and drive. I haven't really noticed if the voices or the thoughts about the curriculum are tied to how motivated I feel on any given week.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had at least two weeks where you continued to hear those voices or feel targeted even when your motivation and energy levels were at their absolute best, rather than your worst?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't experienced those voices or feelings of being targeted during a time when my motivation was at its best. My loss of drive and energy started over a year ago, whereas the voices and feeling targeted only began a few months ago. By the time those things started, I was already feeling at my worst and had been for a long time.\n\nDoctor: When you hear these voices, do they seem to comment on your actions or discuss you among themselves, or do they only speak to you about your feelings of worthlessness and failure?\n\nPatient: Yes, they actually talk to each other about me in the third person rather than speaking directly to me. I'll hear a male and a female voice when I'm alone in my classroom after school, commenting on my teaching and saying things like, \"She's failing them.\""

Reply naturally in English as the Patient to the last Doctor utterance.\nAnswer only the question, in 2–3 sentences maximum.\n\n[Dialogue]\nDoctor: Hello, I am Dr. Smith. Could you please tell me what brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I feel like I’ve lost the drive and interest in everything I used to care about, even my students and the arts stuff I loved. It just feels like the spark is completely gone, and I don't know how to get it back.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt this way for a long time, like several years, or is this a distinct change from how you were feeling a few months ago?\n\nPatient: It hasn't been going on for several years, but it's definitely not brand new either. This loss of drive and motivation has been gradually building for a little over a year, starting when our new school curriculum rolled out. But some of the other distressing experiences, like hearing voices and feeling targeted, only started a few months ago.\n\nDoctor: When you hear these voices or feel targeted, do those experiences occur only during the times you feel severely depressed, or do they also happen when your mood feels relatively stable?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure, as I haven't thought about my feelings in terms of distinct periods of depression versus stable mood. It has just felt like a constant loss of drive and interest this whole time.\n\nDoctor: Looking back over the last few months, have there been any periods where you felt significantly more energetic, irritable, or unusually high-spirited while still experiencing the voices or feelings of being targeted?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't experienced any periods of high energy or feeling high-spirited at all. If anything, it's been the exact opposite—I've just felt completely drained and lacking any motivation.\n\nDoctor: When you hear these voices or feel targeted, do you experience these thoughts and sounds even during weeks where your mood feels slightly better, or do they only happen when your sadness and lack of motivation are at their absolute worst?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure how to answer that, because I don't really experience distinct waves of sadness—it's just been a constant lack of motivation and drive. I haven't really noticed if the voices or the thoughts about the curriculum are tied to how motivated I feel on any given week.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had at least two weeks where you continued to hear those voices or feel targeted even when your motivation and energy levels were at their absolute best, rather than your worst?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't experienced those voices or feelings of being targeted during a time when my motivation was at its best. My loss of drive and energy started over a year ago, whereas the voices and feeling targeted only began a few months ago. By the time those things started, I was already feeling at my worst and had been for a long time.\n\nDoctor: When you hear these voices, do they seem to comment on your actions or discuss you among themselves, or do they only speak to you about your feelings of worthlessness and failure?\n\nPatient: Yes, they actually talk to each other about me in the third person rather than speaking directly to me. I'll hear a male and a female voice when I'm alone in my classroom after school, commenting on my teaching and saying things like, \"She's failing them.\"\n\nDoctor: Have the voices and the feeling of being targeted persisted for at least two weeks in the absence of any significant symptoms of depression or lack of motivation?\n\nPatient: No, they haven't occurred without that lack of motivation. Since my loss of drive has been constant for over a year now, the voices and the feeling of being targeted have only happened alongside it, never in its absence.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that your thoughts are being broadcasted to others, or do you have the sense that your thoughts or actions are being controlled by some outside force or influence?"

I'll hear a male and a female voice when I'm alone in my classroom after school, commenting on my teaching and saying things like, \"She's failing them.\"\n\nDoctor: Have the voices and the feeling of being targeted persisted for at least two weeks in the absence of any significant symptoms of depression or lack of motivation?\n\nPatient: No, they haven't occurred without that lack of motivation. Since my loss of drive has been constant for over a year now, the voices and the feeling of being targeted have only happened alongside it, never in its absence.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that your thoughts are being broadcasted to others, or do you have the sense that your thoughts or actions are being controlled by some outside force or influence?\n\nPatient: No, I've never felt like my thoughts are being broadcasted to other people. I also don't have any sense that some outside force is controlling what I do or think.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced the voices and feelings of being targeted for a continuous period of at least one month, even if the intensity of your low mood or lack of motivation has fluctuated during that time?\n\nPatient: Yes, I have experienced both the voices and the feeling of being targeted continuously for the last few months, which is definitely longer than a month. These experiences have been quite constant recently, even though my overall lack of drive and energy started over a year ago and has fluctuated during that time.\n\nDoctor: When you hear these voices commenting on your teaching, do they occur every day for a significant portion of the day, or do they only happen occasionally?"
  }

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
No, they don't happen every day or throughout the whole day. They only happen occasionally, typically when I'm staying after school in my classroom to straighten up or try to plan.

But some of the other distressing experiences, like hearing voices and feeling targeted, only started a few months ago.\n\nDoctor: When you hear these voices or feel targeted, do those experiences occur only during the times you feel severely depressed, or do they also happen when your mood feels relatively stable?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure, as I haven't thought about my feelings in terms of distinct periods of depression versus stable mood. It has just felt like a constant loss of drive and interest this whole time.\n\nDoctor: Looking back over the last few months, have there been any periods where you felt significantly more energetic, irritable, or unusually high-spirited while still experiencing the voices or feelings of being targeted?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't experienced any periods of high energy or feeling high-spirited at all. If anything, it's been the exact opposite—I've just felt completely drained and lacking any motivation.\n\nDoctor: When you hear these voices or feel targeted, do you experience these thoughts and sounds even during weeks where your mood feels slightly better, or do they only happen when your sadness and lack of motivation are at their absolute worst?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure how to answer that, because I don't really experience distinct waves of sadness—it's just been a constant lack of motivation and drive. I haven't really noticed if the voices or the thoughts about the curriculum are tied to how motivated I feel on any given week.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had at least two weeks where you continued to hear those voices or feel targeted even when your motivation and energy levels were at their absolute best, rather than your worst?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't experienced those voices or feelings of being targeted during a time when my motivation was at its best. My loss of drive and energy started over a year ago, whereas the voices and feeling targeted only began a few months ago. By the time those things started, I was already feeling at my worst and had been for a long time.\n\nDoctor: When you hear these voices, do they seem to comment on your actions or discuss you among themselves, or do they only speak to you about your feelings of worthlessness and failure?\n\nPatient: Yes, they actually talk to each other about me in the third person rather than speaking directly to me. I'll hear a male and a female voice when I'm alone in my classroom after school, commenting on my teaching and saying things like, \"She's failing them.\"\n\nDoctor: Have the voices and the feeling of being targeted persisted for at least two weeks in the absence of any significant symptoms of depression or lack of motivation?\n\nPatient: No, they haven't occurred without that lack of motivation. Since my loss of drive has been constant for over a year now, the voices and the feeling of being targeted have only happened alongside it, never in its absence.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that your thoughts are being broadcasted to others, or do you have the sense that your thoughts or actions are being controlled by some outside force or influence?\n\nPatient: No, I've never felt like my thoughts are being broadcasted to other people. I also don't have any sense that some outside force is controlling what I do or think.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced the voices and feelings of being targeted for a continuous period of at least one month, even if the intensity of your low mood or lack of motivation has fluctuated during that time?\n\nPatient: Yes, I have experienced both the voices and the feeling of being targeted continuously for the last few months, which is definitely longer than a month. These experiences have been quite constant recently, even though my overall lack of drive and energy started over a year ago and has fluctuated during that time.\n\nDoctor: When you hear these voices commenting on your teaching, do they occur every day for a significant portion of the day, or do they only happen occasionally?\n\nPatient: No, they don't happen every day or throughout the whole day. They only happen occasionally, typically when I'm staying after school in my classroom to straighten up or try to plan.\n\nDoctor: Outside of the times you hear these voices, have you noticed any other changes in your thinking, such as your speech becoming disorganized or your behavior becoming notably aimless or erratic?\n\nPatient: Yes, my students and colleagues have pointed out, and I've noticed it too, that I'll suddenly stop mid-lesson and just stand there staring at the whiteboard or fiddling with a stack of papers. I completely lose track of what I was doing for several minutes until someone speaks up to snap me out of it."
  }

These experiences have been quite constant recently, even though my overall lack of drive and energy started over a year ago and has fluctuated during that time.\n\nDoctor: When you hear these voices commenting on your teaching, do they occur every day for a significant portion of the day, or do they only happen occasionally?\n\nPatient: No, they don't happen every day or throughout the whole day. They only happen occasionally, typically when I'm staying after school in my classroom to straighten up or try to plan.\n\nDoctor: Outside of the times you hear these voices, have you noticed any other changes in your thinking, such as your speech becoming disorganized or your behavior becoming notably aimless or erratic?\n\nPatient: Yes, my students and colleagues have pointed out, and I've noticed it too, that I'll suddenly stop mid-lesson and just stand there staring at the whiteboard or fiddling with a stack of papers. I completely lose track of what I was doing for several minutes until someone speaks up to snap me out of it.\n\nDoctor: Besides the moments where you lose track of what you are doing, have you noticed if your speech has become difficult for others to follow, or if you have struggled to keep your thoughts logically connected during conversation?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really noticed any issues with my speech or keeping my thoughts connected during conversations. No one has pointed that specific problem out to me, so I think I'm still able to communicate clearly."

Crucially, the patient denies these psychotic symptoms ever occurring in the absence of the depressive syndrome, and denies mood-incongruent psychotic symptoms or mania. While F20.0 (Paranoid Schizophrenia) is considered due to the nature of the hallucinations, the lack of primary psychotic symptoms independent of mood and the presence of severe, persistent depressive symptoms keep F32.2 (Severe depressive episode without psychotic symptoms—though the patient *does* have psychotic symptoms, the diagnostic hierarchy needs careful consideration) and F25.1 (Schizoaffective disorder, depressive type) in the differential. Subtypes: Fragmented Memory, Generalized Amnesia\n[S072] Negative_Beliefs: Persistent and exaggerated negative beliefs or expectations about oneself, others, or the world. Subtypes: Impulsive Anger, Verbal Aggression, Physical Aggression, Chronic Irritability\n[S078] Reckless_Self_Destructive_Behavior: Reckless or self-destructive behavior pattern that endangers one's safety or health.
